2026-10-11: Investigated the remaining GXAbortFrame symbol rather than substituting a no-op. The original MainLoopFramework alarm recovery invokes GXAbortFrame and immediately submits a recovery GXSetDrawDone from the OS alarm scheduler worker, while the game thread may still be waiting inside synchronous D3D submission. A faithful host implementation needs cancellation-aware ordered submissions, safe command-buffer ownership, and a recovery-fence barrier; directly resetting NativeRenderStream or calling the immediate D3D context from the alarm thread would race. GXAbortFrame remains intentionally unresolved pending that backend work. End-to-end completion remains 0%.
2026-10-11: Implemented the GX watchdog abort/recovery path instead of leaving GXAbortFrame unresolved. Flush now snapshots display-list bytes and releases the shared writer before executing, GXAbortFrame signals cancellation without touching D3D, NativeD3dCommandSubmitter serializes immediate-context submissions and observes cancellation at token fences/operation boundaries, and MainLoopFramework acknowledges recovery after its recovery finish and DrawSyncManager reset preparation. Added bounded waits and an integration test coordinating an alarm thread with a blocked token stream and recovery fence. The D3D abort test passed three consecutive runs; all nine D3D scripts, GX submission/service tests, OS alarm suite, and MainLoopFramework adapter compile passed. Fresh audit: 1,359 providers, zero duplicate symbols, 553 unresolved occurrences / 551 unique (GXAbortFrame resolved). Link remains unsuccessful due audio/DSP and broad runtime dependencies; no executable/gameplay verified. Overall completion remains 0%.
